´ Peru - List of Final Tables

Peru - List of Final Tables

History

On February 27, 1912 the Peruvian Football League was created with teams from Lima (the capital) and Callao (Lima's seaport).  From 1912 until 1921 the
Peruvian League championship was played.

By 1922 the Peruvian Football League was in decline, therefore on August 23, 1922 the Peruvian Football Federation (F.P.F., by its initials in Spanish) was
created. In 1926 and 1927 two unofficial tournaments were played.  Then, in 1928, the first championship organized by the F.P.F. was held. These were
amateur championships until 1951 when the teams turned professional. Until 1965 only teams from Lima and Callao participated.

Then in 1966, four teams from other cities were invited; this was the beginning of the Descentralised Tournaments (Campeonato Descentralizado) involving
teams without any geographical restriction.  There was a major change in 1984 when the number of teams grew (almost three times by 1991) and two tournaments
took place every year: Regional and Descentralizado; both winners then playing for the National Championship.  In 1992 the First Division reverted to its
usual format which remains until now.

Notes:
1. Unless otherwise stated (between brackets) all teams are from Lima.
2. From 1931 until 1942 the points system was  W:3, D:2, L:1, walk-over:0.
3. From 1931 until 1934 the results of a reserve teams league were added as bonus points.
4. Since 1960, the champions qualified to the Copa Libertadores of the following season. Since 1965 a second team also
   qualifies.
5. From 1984 to 1991 a major change occured. As the number of teams increased dramatically, every season was playing in two parts:
   a Regional Tournament which qualified teams for the next stage, the winner also qualified for Copa Libertadores; then the
   Descentralised Tournament where the Championship was contested and another team qualified for Copa Libertadores.
6. Since 1992 one team qualifies to Copa Conmebol.
7. Since 1995 the points system is W:3, D:1, L:0.
8. Since 1999 a third team qualifies for Copa Libertadores.
9. Since 2002 one team qualifies for Copa Sudamericana.

Championship playoff: Universitario de Deportes - Alianza Lima 2-1

Topscorer: Teodoro Fernández (Universitario de Deportes), 9 goals.
Notes:
1. Alianza was the regular champion, but as Universitario protested the rules a playoff took place. After
   winning this playoff Universitario won the 1934 Championship.  
2. Circolo Sportivo Italiano disbanded after 3 matches. Hence, their 5 remaining matches were awarded to their opponents, Circolo
   gaining 0 pts.
3. No team was promoted and First Division was reduced to 5 teams for 1935.

1936

No tournament took place, because of the participation of the Peruvian National Team in the 1936 Olympic Games soccer tournament.

   their final place - were relegated to 1967 Copa Perú; this tournament promoted 3 teams to 1967 First Division. This explains why in
   spite of being relegated, Alfonso Ugarte and Octavio Espinosa played in 1967 First Division.
2. Miguel Grau (Piura) changed name to Atlético Grau (Piura) for 1967.

   title and bottom 7 played for the relegation. They carried their regular season records and played an additional round (6
   further matches). 
2. Points system: in the first half the regular season (13 matches) the usual W:2, D:1, L:0; in the second half of the regular 
   season (13 matches) and in the groups stage (6 matches): W:4, D:2, L:0. 
3. Four teams were promoted, as First Division grew to 16 teams for 1971.
4. Deportivo SIMA is for Servicios Industriales de la MArina.

Notes:
1. The season was divided into 3 stages.  The first stage was contested in two groups: Metropolitan (teams from Lima and Callao)
   and Regional (teams from the rest of the country); each group winner qualified for the Championship Group. The second stage was the
   Descentralised (league tournament); the top 4 qualified for the Championship Group and the bottom 2 were relegated. Championship
   Group was contested by 6 teams in Lima; neither team carried their previous records.
2. Four teams were promoted as First Division grew to 18 teams for 1973.

2. Sportivo Huracán were relegated because of a rule stating that the worst placed team from any city with more than one representative
   (in this case Arequipa) was to be relegated. This rule did not apply to Lima clubs.
3. Eight teams were promoted in order to have more "provincial" teams; therefore First Divison grew to 22 teams for 1974.

Topscorer: José Leyva (Alfonso Ugarte), 25 goals.
Notes:
1. The top 6 teams from the First Stage qualified to the Championship Group (in Lima) carrying their whole season record.
2. As the First Division was reduced to 16 teams for 1976, no team was promoted and two teams were relegated. The criteria for
   relegation: Atlético Grau as the last placed team; Unión Tumán as the worst team from a Department (Lambayeque) with two teams
   (the other was Juan Aurich). This rule didn't apply for Lima clubs. 
3. A playoff match for 2nd place (qualifing for the Copa Libertadores) was necessary.

Notes:
1. The season was divided into 3 stages.  The Prelimnary Tournament contested in two groups (home and away matches plus 2 inter-group
   matches); each group winner qualified for the Championship Group. The second stage was the Descentralised (league tournament); the
   top 4 qualified for the Championship Group and the bottom two played for relegation. Championship Group was contested by 6 teams in
   home and away matches; teams carried their Descentralised Tournament record.
2. Although finishing fifth in Descentralised, Universitario qualified for Championship Group, as Alianza had already qualified in
   Preliminary Tournament.
3. Deportivo Municipal and Cienciano played a third relegation playoff on a neutral ground, as each team won one match after home
   and away matches.

   Division grew to 17 teams for 1983. Deportivo Junín changed name to Huancayo F.C. for 1983.

Notes:                               
1. Teams that not qualified for Descentralised Tournament played on the Intermediary Division against teams from Second Division to
   gain promotion for next year.    
2. Every champion group plus top 4 from Metropolitan Group, played the Regional Playoffs in Lima for a Copa Libertadores place. As
   Metropolitan champion, Alianza Lima had a bye to Semifinals. Regional Champion will playoff against Descentralised Champion for the 1985
   Championship.
3. Juventud La Joya and Deportivo COOPTRIP played off for a place in Descentralised Tournament.
4. Deportivo COOPTRIP is for "COOPerativa TRIPlayera".
5. Huancayo F.C. reverted to their original name Deportivo Junín for Descentralised Tournament.

   fifth placed team in Metropolitan group.
3. Regional champion will playoff against Descentralised champion for the National Championship.
4. During Preliminary Round of Regional Playoffs, the match Coronel Bolognesi - San Agustín, was replayed after Bolognesi played the first
   match under protest.
5. For 1987, Juventud La Joya merged with Centro Iqueño, changing name to La Joya - Iqueño.

Notes:
1. The Intermedio Tournament "Middle-Season Tournament" was played at half season, while the National Team participated at Copa América
   and WC Qualifiers. The winner qualified for Copa Conmebol. Eventually, Deportivo Muncipal Intermedio winners, resigned their place and
   Sporting Cristal as Liguilla runner-up qualified for Copa Conmebol.
2. As regular season runner-up, Alianza Lima had a 1 point bonus for Liguilla which was played in Lima.

Liguilla
                                       P -  W -  D -  L - GF - GA - PTS  
 1. Alianza Lima                       3 -  2 -  1 -  0 -  5 -  2 -   5  [To Copa Libertadores]
 2. Deportivo Sipesa (Chimbote)        3 -  1 -  2 -  0 -  4 -  3 -   4

Topscorer: Flavio Maestri (Sporting Cristal), 25 goals.
Promoted: Atlético Torino, Unión Huaral.
Notes:                                 
1. Apertura Tournament was played at the beginning of the season, the winner gained Peru´s place for Copa Conmebol and a 2 points bonus
   for regular season. As at the end of the season, Sporting Cristal won the championship, as Apertura runner-up, Ciclista Lima
   qualified for Copa Conmebol.        
2. León and Deportivo Sipesa qualified to Liguilla, during the playoffs, because of their best regular season record.
3. As regular season runner-up, Universitario de Deportes had a 1 point bonus for Liguilla which was played in Lima.

Topscorer: Julio de Andrade "Julinho" (Sporting Cristal), 23 goals.   
Promoted: La Loretana, Guardia Republicana. 
Notes:
1. The top 8 teams from the First Stage qualified to the Championship Group played in home and away matches, carrying their whole season
   records.
2. On the Championship Group, Alianza Lima and Universitario de Deportes, both had 1 point deducter from their match that ended 0-0. That
   match was called-off because of troubles from both teams supporters.
3. A second place playoff was necessary as two teams tied second place on points.
4. Deportivo Sipesa change its name to Deportivo Pesquero for 1996.

Liguilla
                                       P -  W -  D -  L - GF - GA - PTS
 1. Sporting Cristal                   5 -  4 -  1 -  0 -  9 -  1 -  13  [To Copa Libertadores]
 2. Universitario de Deportes          5 -  2 -  2 -  1 - 12 -  4 -   8
 3. Melgar F.B.C. (Arequipa)           5 -  2 -  2 -  1 -  4 -  4 -   8  [To Copa Conmebol]
 4. Cienciano (Cusco)                  5 -  2 -  0 -  3 -  8 - 10 -   6
 5. Alianza Atlético (Sullana)         5 -  1 -  2 -  2 -  8 - 11 -   5
 6. Deportivo Municipal                5 -  0 -  1 -  4 -  5 - 16 -   1
 
Topscorer: Ricardo Zegarra (Alianza Atlético), 17 goals.
Promoted: Juan Aurich, Lawn Tennis F.C.. 
Notes:
1. The season was divided in two tournaments: Apertura and Clausura. Both winners were supposed to playoff for the title; the winner
   being the Champion and the looser playing against Liguilla winner for the second place to Copa Libertadores. As Alianza Lima won both
   tournaments they were automatically Champions and Liguilla winner qualified directly to Copa Libertadores.
2. The aggregated table (sum of both tournaments) was used to determine teams for Liguilla and relegated teams.
3. Four teams were relegated as First Division was reduced to 12 teams for 1998.
4. In Liguilla, the home team was the one which a better result considering the matches played between both teams in Apertura and
   Clausura.
5. Melgar F.B.C. qualified for Copa Conmebol after Universitario de Deportes resigned their place.

Universitario de Deportes  [2000 Champion]   
 
Topscorer: Eduardo Esidio (Universitario de Deportes), 37 goals.

Notes:
1. The season was divided in two tournaments: Apertura and Clausura. Both winners were meanto to qualify for Copa Libertadores and play-off for
   the season title. As Universitario won both tournaments, there were the overall season champions without any play-off.
2. Because the same team won both Apertura and Clausura; both runner-ups in each tournament also qualified for Copa Libertadores.
   These teams played a "Second place playoff" to establish which team will enter as Peru 2 and Peru 3 in Copa Libertadores.
3. Before the season started Deportivo Pesquero moved to Huancayo and the team was renamed Deportivo Wanka.
4. U.P.A.O. stands for Universidad Particular Antenor Orrego.
5. The aggregated table (sum of both tournaments) was used to determine teams relegated teams.

Alianza Lima  [2001 Champion]   
 
Topscorer: Jorge Ramírez (Deportivo Wanka), 21 goals.
Promoted: Coronel Bolognesi
Notes:
1. The season was divided in two tournaments: Apertura and Clausura. Both winners qualified for Copa Libertadores and played off for
   the season title. Both Apertura and Clausura runner-ups played off for the third Copa Libertadores spot.
2. There were playoffs in both the Apertura and Clausura tournaments, as each time, two teams tied in points, and goal difference was not taken in consideration.
3. There was playoff ("11th place playoff") to determine the team to play the Relegation Playoff, as tow teams tied the 11th place in points in the Aggregated table
   and goal difference was not take in consideration.
4. Before the season started Deportivo U.P.A.O. merged with Sport Coopsol (from 2nd Division); the team was renamed Coopsol Trujillo.
5. The aggregated table (sum of both tournaments) was used to determine teams relegated teams.

Sporting Cristal  [2002 Champion]   
 
Topscorer: Luis Artime (Melgar F.B.C.), 24 goals.
Promoted: Atlético Universidad, Unión Huaral
Notes:
1. The season was divided in two tournaments: Apertura and Clausura. Both winners qualified for Copa Libertadores and were 
   supposed to play off for the season title. However, the playoff didn’t took place as Universitario did not finish in the top-4
   of Clausura. As Cristal finished in the top-4 of Apertura, they were the season champions.
2. The team with the best aggregated score –not counting Apertura and Clausura winners- also qualified for Copa Libertadores.
3. There was a playoff in the Apertura tournament as both teams tied in points, and goal difference was not taken in consideration.
4. Alianza Lima and Alianza Atlético had 3 points deducted from Clausura and the Aggregated table for failing to carry out
   doping tests in round 22 of Apertura.
5. The aggregated table (sum of both tournaments) was used to determine teams relegated teams. There was a Relegation Playoff as two
   teams tied the 11th place in the Aggregated Table and goal difference was not took in consideration.

11. Deportivo Wanka (Huancayo)       15 -  3 -  4 -  8 – 11 - 24 -  13  [2-1, 0-1]

Clausura tournament was disrupted by a players strike after Round 15. At that time a match between Cienciano and Atl.Universidad
was still pending (eventually it never took place). Rounds 16 and 17 (except 3 games) were played with U-20 players and those
results (that are between brackets) were eventually cancelled. Because the strike was not called-off (it eventually did in Jan 04),
the Peruvian Soocer Federation ruled that Clausura tournament and the overall season were over.
Because Alianza Lima was leading Clausura (and also leading the Aggregated table –see below-) they were named to playoff against Apertura
Champions for the 2003 Season Championship; by means of this, they also qualified for Copa Libertadores.

Copa Libertadores / Copa Sudamericana Qualifying Playoff:
 
After the srike events, the Peruvian Soccer Federation ruled that a playoff tournament had to be played to determine the
third team to qualify for Copa Libertadores and the two teams to qualify for Copa Sudamericana.
It was decided that teams placed 3rd to 6th in the Aggregated table should take part in this playoff. However because the
7th placed team (Cienciano) had 1 less game and could have passed the 6th placed team (Unión Huaral), had they won that game,
a preliminary playoff was played between those 2 teams. Also, Sport Boys resigned their right to participate in the playoff.
Eventually a 3-team playoff took place in Jan-Feb 04
 
Preliminary Round
Unión Huaral – Cienciano    2-2 / 0-2  [Cienciano qualified to Playoff]
Playoff (in Lima)
                                    P - W - D - L - F - A - PTS
 1. Cienciano (Cusco)               2 – 2 - 0 - 0 – 3 - 0 -  6   [To Copa Libertadores]  
 2. Alianza Atlético (Sullana)      2 – 1 - 0 - 1 – 1 - 1 -  3   [To Copa Sudamericana]
 3. Coronel Bolognesi (Tacna)       2 – 0 - 0 - 2 – 0 - 3 -  0   [To Copa Sudamericana]
 
Topscorer: Luis Bonnet (Sporting Cristal), 20 goals.
Promoted: Sport Coopsol, Universidad César Vallejo
Notes:
1. The season was disrupted by a players strike after Round 15 of Clausura. Rounds 16 and 17 (except 3 games) were played with
   U-20 players and those results were eventually cancelled. After the strike wasn’t called-out (it eventually happened in 
   Jan 04), the season was ruled to be over by the Peruvian Soccer Federation.
2. The season was divided in two tournaments: Apertura and Clausura. Both winners were due to qualify for Copa Libertadores and were 
   supposed to play off for the season title. The team with the best aggregated score –not counting Apertura and Clausura winners- was
   meant to get the third spot for Copa Libertadores. However because of the above mentioned events (see note 1) all this was modified.
3. No relegation in 2003. First Division increases to 14 teams in 2004.

Championship Matches
====================
as Clausura Winners Coronel Bolognesi did not finish in the top-6
of the Apertura (nor did Apertura winners San Martin do so in the
Clausura), no championship playoff is held; the title is won by
the best team in the aggregate table.
